# Analysis changelog — TRACKER AUDIT TRAIL

**Scope, decided 2026-08-15 (D0.5).** This file records *why tracker state changed*: every
confidence move, every deferral upheld or overturned, every uncertainty filed or resolved,
every correction to a previous claim. It is the one record `git log` cannot replace — git
tells you what changed, not why a C-level moved or why a deferral still stands.

**It is NOT a commit log.** Do not measure it against commit count; do not try to make it
complete against the repo's history. Trying to be both is what made it drift twice, and
per-lane docs plus `git log` cover the other half.

**Ordering: NEWEST FIRST.** One entry per line, starting `YYYY-MM-DD`. New entries are
**prepended immediately below the `<!-- ENTRIES -->` marker**, never appended to the end.
Several skill docs said "append" while the file was prepend-ordered; that mismatch is part
of how `2dee9c67` (2026-07-30) came to *overwrite* the file, destroying 477 entries
(recovered 2026-08-15).

**This file is append-only in spirit: never rewrite or truncate it.** If an entry was
wrong, add a new entry correcting it — do not edit history.

**Do not cite entries by line number.** Prepending shifts every line below. Cite by date
plus subject (e.g. "CHANGELOG 2026-06-18 physics-chain C4-DEBT DISPOSITION"). Existing
line-number citations elsewhere in the repo predate this rule and are shifted by the
length of this header.

Entries before 2026-06-15 live in `archive/CHANGELOG_pre20260615.md`.

<!-- ENTRIES -->

2026-09-01  B-0003 / 004726f0  RESOLVED, and the filed DIAGNOSIS was WRONG ON BOTH COUNTS - corrected in place rather than quietly closed. B-0003 claimed (i) 0x004726f0's ST0 return feeds record byte+0x27 and (ii) a void-typed port breaks shipped C3 correctness, filed as highest severity. Checked against the binary (DecompPC 0x00412cf0, pool8, read-only): byte +0x27 is `uVar2 = FUN_004a2c48(); *(undefined1*)((int)param_1 + 0x27) = uVar2` - the QPC tick, NOT 0x004726f0 - and our port already wrote it correctly as `color_bytes[3] = fn_tick() & 0xff`. The original DISCARDS 0x004726f0's return (bare statement `FUN_004726f0(&local_c,&local_18);`), so discarding it is faithful. And the installed reimpl at 0x004726f0 (PromoLoop_sessionB.cpp Dot4726f0) was ALREADY declared `float __cdecl` and RH_ScopedInstall'd, so nothing shipped with a void-typed 0x004726f0 and the C3 row was never wrong. WHAT WAS REAL, and it is worth having: a LATENT x87 leak in a CALLER - UtilMid.cpp declared `typedef void (__cdecl*)(float*,float*)` and called through it, and a void-typed indirect call never emits the FSTP, so each call leaked one x87 register ([[x87-st0-float10-fnptr-void-leak]], the class that froze the sim in the ai lane). Severity LOWER than filed because the caller LabelTrailRecordAppend (0x00412cf0) is MASS-DISABLED since 2026-05-24 - its RH_ScopedInstall is commented out - so the leak could not fire in the shipped .asi; it would have bitten on re-enable. FIXED: typedef declared float, return bound to a discarded local so the pop is emitted; build clean both targets; path2 spot-check GREEN (rw_matrix_invert, text_ctrl_code_remap, vec3_centroid). PROCESS LESSON, since this is the second wrong inference corrected today: the filing generalised from ONE caller's FCOM to 'every ST0-dependent caller' without checking the caller it actually named. The return IS consumed somewhere - just not where the note said. No confidence level moved.

2026-09-01  00415200  AiVehicle0ZeroProgressGuard  RESOLVED AS STEADY-STATE UNREACHABLE - stays C2, and the r5 question is now CLOSED with a mechanism rather than an empirical shrug. area-ai r7 mapped the FUN_00415220 jump table (re/analysis/ai_00415220_jumptable_reachability.md, addresses read from MASHED.exe.unpatched). Dispatch is JMP [EAX*0x4+0x41582c] at 0x004152ca with index=(value-7), bounded CMP EAX,0xc / JA 0x00415822. The 13-entry table puts the three 0x00415200 calls in arms value {7, 9, 0xb} and the four 0x00415190 calls in arms {0xa, 0xc, 0x10, 0x11} - which is precisely why r6 logged 129+ calls in a plain race and r5 logged none, and it confirms the correction filed earlier today (FUN_00415220 DOES run; my original 'sub-tree unreached' reasoning was wrong). THE REAL FINDING is a dead-code argument: the prologue sets [ESI+0x89a520]=0xbb8 on EVERY tick where the accumulator [ESI+0x89a51c] < 0xbb8 (0x0041527e), and [0x89a520] is cleared to 0 ONLY together with [0x89a51c]=0 by the three resets FUN_00416250/FUN_00416a30/FUN_00417da0 (all called from FUN_00418560). The accumulator steps up from 0 by the per-frame delta [0x007f1008] one frame at a time, so every route to [0x89a51c] >= 0xbb8 passes through frames that re-arm [0x89a520]=0xbb8. '[0x89a520]==0' and 'accumulator >= 0xbb8' are therefore MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE in steady per-frame stepping; the only opening is the first tick after a reset carrying a single-frame delta >= 0xbb8, i.e. a load/hitch stall. PARENT DECISION: option (b) - accept the reachability finding, keep C2, do NOT build a contrived-state cache-setter lane to force the call. Forcing it would witness the body in a state the game does not reach in steady play, which is weak evidence for a race-witnessed C3; the route is recorded should the row ever be needed. This is NOT a defect claim: the reimplementation is a faithful transcription, it is simply not exercised. No semantic assigned to any mode value or to the constants 0xbb8 / 0xea60.

2026-09-01  00415200  CORRECTION to the same-day refusal note - the refusal STANDS, the REASON was WRONG. Earlier today I recorded that 0x00415200 is unreachable because 'the sub-tree is unreached in a plain track-0 4-car race', inferred from its sole caller FUN_00415220 apparently not running. The r6 race disproves it: 0x00415190 has the SAME sole caller and logged 129+ calls in a plain track-0 4-car race, so FUN_00415220 DOES execute and my inference was wrong. Actual mechanism from the disassembly: FUN_00415220 dispatches through a JUMP TABLE at 0x004152ca (JMP dword ptr [EAX*0x4 + 0x41582c]) with index (*[EAX] - 7) bounded by CMP EAX,0xc / JA 0x00415822 - a switch over ~13 behaviour-mode values. The three 0x00415200 calls (0x004152ed, 0x004153d1, 0x0041543d) sit in arms a plain race does not select, and 0x004152ed is further gated by TEST [ESI+0x89a524] and [ESI+0x89a520]; the 0x00415190 calls sit in arms that ARE selected. So the blocker is switch-arm coverage, not reachability. Recorded as a correction rather than an edit because the original claim was already committed and a later session would otherwise trust it. NO semantic assigned to any mode value. This is exactly why the differential control (a second hook logging in the same race) is worth running before concluding anything from an absent log.

Actual mechanism from the disassembly: FUN_00415220 dispatches through a JUMP TABLE at 0x004152ca (JMP dword ptr [EAX*0x4 + 0x41582c]) with index (*[EAX] - 7) bounded by CMP EAX,0xc / JA 0x00415822 - a switch over ~13 behaviour-mode values. The three 0x00415200 calls (0x004152ed, 0x004153d1, 0x0041543d) sit in arms a plain race does not select, and 0x004152ed is further gated by TEST [ESI+0x89a524] and [ESI+0x89a520]; the 0x00415190 calls sit in arms that ARE selected. So the blocker is switch-arm coverage, not reachability. Recorded as a correction rather than an edit because the original claim was already committed and a later session would otherwise trust it. NO semantic assigned to any mode value. This is exactly why the differential control (a second hook logging in the same race) is worth running before concluding anything from an absent log.

2026-08-31  U-9062 and U-9063 RESOLVED — ONE root cause, and it is NOT a lighting bug: `Clump_Exclude_From_World(N)` was read as "do not load clump N at all". City's COURSE.LUA excludes indices 6-13 (Build01-04, Standard, road.dff at 11, Trunk, water.dff at 13) and Dump's clump 9 is Road.dff, so the road SURFACE WAS MISSING, not dark — and Dump's "blown out white sky" was never a sky defect, it is the white background showing through the hole where the road belongs. DECODED from the binary rather than inferred (analyzeHeadless read-only on pool slot Mashed_pool14; Ghidra MCP was not wired into the session, and analyzeHeadless is Ghidra on the same binary so this satisfies the no-documentation-fallback rule): the Lua table at 0x00440bc0..0x00440d40 binds the string at 0x005cde94 to handler 0x0047aa20 via registrar 0x0047b980; that handler's entire body is desc[0xd4 + idx*4] = 1 and it never unloads or clears the filename slot. Course::CreateFromDescription (0x00479330) loads every clump with a non-empty filename, gating on the name and never on the flag. The flag gates exactly ONE call, in the walk from `add edi,0xd4` (0x00479d93): cmp dword ptr [edi],0 (0x00479da6) / jnz (0x00479da9) / call 0x004e4450(world, clump) (0x00479db3), which links the clump frame and runs RpClumpForAllAtomics/RpClumpForAllLights to register it INTO the world; that the first arg is the RpWorld is not assumed, [course+0x105d4] is the same field RpWorldAddLight is called on. get-first-atomic (0x00474fd0) runs unconditionally at 0x00479dbe and its handle lands at +0x120 either way. So an excluded clump IS loaded and DOES keep a live atomic; it is only kept out of the world registration. Fixed by loading them as ordinary props (we render props explicitly, not via a world pass); default ON, MASHED_TRACK_SKIP_EXCLUDED=1 reverts. Re-measured on the shipping default with NO env vars rather than inheriting the opt-in numbers: City 25.43 -> 11.23 (crushed-black 57.88% -> 12.36% vs original 11.32%), Dump 84.78 -> 9.62, Arctic 18.85 unchanged, TRAINING 15.45 -> 15.40 so the headline survives even though it now loads and folds three water clumps. Ruled out first: not a librw-vs-D3D9 asymmetry (both renderers agreed within 0.4 points), not the ambient fold, not texture resolution, not the world path. ALSO CORRECTS a claim made earlier the same day in verify/geomlight_waterfold/RESULT.md: the reason no water clump was built on the other water tracks was NOT "their water is world/BSP geometry", it was this same skip — every water clump in the game except Arctic's sea.dff is excluded, which is why Arctic was the one surface the water fold could act on. NEW DEBT booked not buried: water now loads on seven tracks, so the fold fires there for the first time; Forest (1.84% mask) and SuperG (0.86%) get materially folded water with no pose-matched reference, while sands/Storm/Warzone/City/rouabout measure 0.00% — and those zeros are now genuine "fired and was inert" rather than "never ran". 2026-08-31  GEOMLIGHT WATER FOLD — the ambient-fold scope key rewritten from a geometry-flag test to an ASSET-NAME test, and DEFAULTED ON. The flags-only prototype (numTexCoordSets<=1 && !lit && prelit) was a geometry-CLASS key, not a water key: it also matched a City awning and lamppost and a Dump skydome sliver, pushing them further from the original than no fold at all (City orig 73.9 -> 148.4, Dump 53.5 -> 92.5) while correctly fixing the Arctic sea. No RW flag combination separates those cases because they are genuinely the same class, so the discriminator had to come from the asset. DffModel::Parse only receives a blob, so DffModel::source_name was added and is filled by TrackRenderer's load_prop (the one site that knows the name); RwSceneBuild::ModelIsWaterAsset does a case-insensitive PREFIX match on SEA/WATER/LAKE/RIVER, ANDed with the existing flag class — ANDed, so the new scope is strictly NARROWER than the prototype and every track that was already a no-op stays one by construction. MIST.DFF is deliberately excluded (fog billboard, not a water surface). Verified against pose-matched originals, luma over the fold mask: Arctic s8 mask 69.63% orig 28.0 unfolded 9.1 folded 29.8; Arctic s14 mask 58.37% orig 32.6 unfolded 9.8 folded 27.2; City / Dump / TRAINING mask 0.00%, exact no-ops. TRAINING imgdiff 8x6 is 15.45 on both arms, byte-identical, so the geomlight road win is untouched. The measurement harness was validated BEFORE use by replaying it over the broadcheck's own City captures, reproducing that published row within 0.1 luma. The fold is now attributed BY NAME in the log rather than inferred from a mask: BuildClump logs dff= and water_asset= per clump, and across the 10-run set exactly one asset matched (sea.dff) while all 32 others logged 0, including objects01/02.dff, Lamposts.dff, skydome*.dff and Road.dff. Default flipped ON with MASHED_LIBRW_AMBFOLD_SEA=0 as the off-switch. Standalone renderer only, no RVA moved, no confidence level moved, hooks.csv untouched. Evidence verify/geomlight_waterfold/RESULT.md.

FUN_0043dfd0 zeroes seven enable slots at 0x0043e3ff..0x0043e417 then walks the screen's ff<op>0000 stream setting one slot per bare opcode (0x0043e420..0x0043e48f): ff11->[ESP+0x20], ff0b->[ESP+0x24], ff0c->[ESP+0x28], ff0d->[ESP+0x2c], ff0e->[ESP+0x30], ff10/ff23->EDX, and decisively ff12->[ESP+0x1c] = the ACTION 6 enable (gate 0x0043fef9) and ff33->[ESP+0x34] = the ACTION 7 enable (gate 0x004409c8). FUN_0040fc00 at 0x0040fe46 gates the clock update on DAT_0063ba8c -- "CMP dword ptr [0x0063ba8c],0x7; JZ skip" -- so the clock does NOT advance while that state global is 7, and the decompilation shows states 10..11 gated the same way earlier in the function (line 61: DAT_0063ba8c == 7 || (9 < DAT_0063ba8c && DAT_0063ba8c < 0xc)). The increment is ESI = 50 per tick. A fixed-window rate fit therefore under-reports by exactly the fraction of the window spent frozen, and that fraction GROWS with the speed multiplier because the race reaches round transitions sooner in wall time. MEASURED frozen fraction: 15% at 1.5x, 38% at 2.0x. That is the entire "speed-up saturates near 1.45x" artifact

2026-08-30  MOD SHIPPED (with a measured ceiling): MASHED_SPEED game-speed multiplier, mashedmod/src/qol_asi/mashed_qol.cpp. Scales the measured delta fed to the quantizer FUN_00493480, Q16.16 fixed point, applied BEFORE the stock 4-tick hitch clamp so the clamp still bounds the engine step; the existing sub-unit remainder carry keeps fractional precision. Unparseable or <=0 falls back to 1.0, never 0. MEASURED with the new re/frida/speed_probe.py (race clock DAT_007f0ff4 vs wall clock, phase 3): 0.5 -> 0.500x EXACT (1499.6 units/s, accumulator alternating 0/25 and units 0/50 exactly as the quantizer math predicts), 1.0 -> 1.000x (2999.0). SLOWDOWN IS EXACT. SPEED-UP SATURATES: 1.5 -> 1.448x, 2.0 -> only 1.21x @60 cap / 1.25x @165. Asking 2x puts 2 ticks on every frame, the frame loop falls behind, and the stock kUnitsCap 4-tick clamp then truncates -- which is why 2.0 measures WORSE than 1.5. Clamp left intact deliberately; values >1.5 are accepted but not delivered, and that is documented in the header rather than hidden
2026-08-30  TRAP, cost a full debugging cycle: mashed_re_dev.asi SILENTLY DISABLES MASHED_SPEED. FrameDispatch.cpp installs RH_ScopedInstall(FpsDiscretise, 0x00493480), a verbatim port that REPLACES the quantizer and calls FUN_00493390 directly, bypassing the call site mashed_qol.asi retargets. Ultimate-ASI-Loader loads EVERY .asi in original/, so with both deployed the dev hook wins and the multiplier has no effect and no error. Diagnosed by sampling the sub-frame accumulator DAT_007719d4: it read 0 at 0.5x when it must alternate 0/25, proving the scaled units never reached the quantizer. 2026-08-30  U-9058 RESOLVED, same day it was filed. The RwCamera frame basis is written by Camera::InitWithMatrix (0x00442a20 @ 0x00442a4e), a 16-dword copy of a matrix composed at 0x0089650c by FUN_00445aa0 (0x00445aa0, already C2); Camera::Apply also writes frame+0x10 and its output is OVERWRITTEN, which is why the controller fields never reconciled. The 28.76deg roll is FUN_00445aa0's THIRD rotation, about the camera's own forward column: roll_deg = 30.0 * sin((DAT_007f101c & 0x3ff) * 2*pi/1024). Constants read from the binary: _DAT_005cc72c = exactly 30.0 (degrees, RwMatrixRotate 0x004c4d20 is C4 and takes degrees), _DAT_005ce17c = 0.006135742 = 2*pi/1024 to the bit. Corroborated by the June C2 note on 0x00446520 ('sinusoidal jitter'). HARNESS CONSEQUENCE: 60deg of roll swing means a single-frame parity capture must transplant a basis from the SAME frame as the reference. Evidence re/analysis/race_camera/U9058_frame_ltm_writer_20260830.md, race/camframe 96d2e83a.

2026-08-30  0x080D voice banks now WRITABLE (equal-length replacement) -- audio read+write loop closed (no RVA, no ladder move)  New tool re/tools/rws_voice_replace.py: IMA ADPCM encoder + in-place clip replacement, companion to rws_voice_index.py. The encoder mirrors the decoder exactly (low nibble first) and quantises against the RECONSTRUCTED predictor rather than the raw input, which is what keeps it in lockstep with the decoder. DELIBERATE DESIGN CONSTRAINT: replacement is IN PLACE AT THE IDENTICAL BYTE LENGTH -- pad with silence, refuse if too long -- so the cut table, the header and every other clip are untouched. Variable length was NOT attempted on purpose: it would mean rewriting the cut table and whatever else encodes totals, and the header carries fields that are NOT understood (the two large dwords at +0x14/+0x18, order 2.8e7, plus six per-record dwords beyond length/offset). Rewriting those blind risks a bank the game mis-reads in ways a byte diff would not catch. Equal-length swap touches none of them and is enough for a voice swap. THREE VALIDATIONS, all PASS: (1) roundtrip read+rewrite with no change is BYTE-IDENTICAL; (2) re-encoding clip 5's own extracted WAV produces exactly 40,960 bytes -- the original length, zero padding -- which is strong evidence the encoder matches the format; (3) every other clip stays byte-identical and the modified bank re-parses with tiles=YES, 98 clips, groups [13,25,25,35]. Encode->decode mean abs error 386.8 = 1.18% of full scale, which is ordinary ADPCM round-trip loss, not a defect -- IMA is lossy. SCOPE: this closes the read+write loop for 0x080D VOICE banks specifically. TXD (textures) and DFF (models) still have no writer, so the broader asset-modding gap identified at the start of this session is narrowed further but still open on those two.
2026-08-30  0x080D voice banks are now fully READABLE: clip index + cut points + extraction (no RVA, no ladder move)  re/tools/rws_voice_index.py gained the CUT TABLE and a working --extract. Format: a second table at header offset 120, stride 0x20, 98 entries, LENGTH at +0x00 and BYTE OFFSET at +0x04 into the 0x080F stream. PROVEN not fitted -- in every bank tested the 98 clips TILE THE DATA SECTION EXACTLY (off[i]+len[i] == off[i+1] for all i, last clip ends precisely at the data size, sum of lengths == data size: red 1,832,960; gold 6,123,520; melon 1,884,160). Lengths are multiples of 0x2800 = 10240, the block size that also sits verbatim at header +0x34. The tool refuses to cut if the tiling check fails. STATE-RESET CAVEAT, measured rather than hand-waved: IMA is stateful, and decoding a clip standalone from (pred=0, idx=0) does NOT match decoding it as a slice of the continuous stream -- 97 of 98 clips differ. But the differences are entirely a CONTIGUOUS RUN AT THE CLIP START, after which the decoders converge and stay bit-identical: RD_2 651/102400 all in [0,650] (14.8 ms at 44.1 kHz), RD_3 305/61440 all in [0,304], RD_6 75/81920 all in [0,74], RD_13 219/20480 all in [0,218]. That is predictor convergence, not a wrong cut -- the cut points are independently proven by the tiling. Which convention the GAME uses is NOT established, so the first ~15 ms of an extracted clip is approximate. --extract runs this check on every clip and reports failures rather than silently writing. SCOPE, stated so this is not over-claimed: this closes the READ side of the audio asset gap for 0x080D voice banks -- clips can be enumerated, named, and cut. It does NOT close the WRITE side. There is still no IMA encoder and no 0x080D container writer, so a modified or replacement voice line cannot yet be put back into the game. The asset-modding gap identified at the start of this session (no TXD/RWS/DFF writer) is therefore narrowed, not closed.

(1) LOAD-TIME VALIDATION, previously undocumented: FUN_00498510's second pass copies the default record aside, lets FUN_004971b0 read the file over it, and accepts it ONLY IF record[+0x000] == 6 AND strcmp(record+0x004, default+0x004) == 0 -- the DEVICE NAME MUST MATCH -- otherwise it copies the defaults back and discards the file. A wrong name does not error; the load succeeds, is silently overwritten, and looks exactly like the file never being read. This also narrows U-9048: the constant 6 at +0x000 has no ordinary reader but IS consumed by this comparison, so it gates the load. (2) SLOT ASSIGNMENT IS NOT FIXED. FUN_00498510 gives slots 0..joycount-1 to joypads and slot joycount to the keyboard. On this machine slot 0 and slot 1 are both joypads named 'Keychron Link ' and the KEYBOARD IS SLOT 2 (name 'Keyboard'), because a Keychron keyboard enumerates as TWO DirectInput GAMECTRL devices. So contcfg0.bin is a joypad config here. My first attempt wrote a keyboard record to slot 0 and failed BOTH claims for this single reason -- correctly rejected by the name check, and the keyboard-bitmap injection could never drive a type-1 slot anyway. Any tool must read the live table to find the keyboard slot rather than assuming 0. NOTE ON original\: the test installs a contcfg<N>.bin into original\ (a file the game itself creates), refuses to overwrite a pre-existing one, and removes it afterwards unless --keep. 2026-08-21  CORRECTION to today's D2 entry: the real-physics car is NOT "75x too fast" — it is SLOWER than the scaffold, and the defect localises to steer -> lateral velocity  I READ A TELEMETRY FIELD AS THE CAR'S SPEED WHEN IT IS THE CHAIN'S INTERNAL VELOCITY, AND SHIPPED THAT INTO ROADMAP BEFORE CHECKING  Correcting entry, per this file's rule that history is not edited. The earlier entry today and the first version of D2_REALPHYS_REMEASURE_2026-08-21.md claimed the ported chain runs "75x too fast", from PLAY-DEMO reporting speed=1500.00 against the scaffold's 20.11. WRONG. Deriving velocity from the logged positions instead of trusting the field: real physics moves at 11.86, 12.31, 11.85 units/s (|dpos|/dt) while REPORTING 1500.00 — a 122-127x discrepancy between the field and the motion — whereas on the scaffold the field and the motion agree to 1.0x (17.87 reported vs 17.09 actual, 20.11 vs 19.76). So PLAY-DEMO's speed= reports the chain's INTERNAL saturated velocity, and only coincides with real motion on the scaffold path. The car under MASHED_REAL_PHYSICS actually travels ~11.9 units/s against the scaffold's ~20: SLOWER, not faster. ROADMAP §D2 corrected in place; the analysis note carries the correction with the arithmetic. CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY by coupling_diag.log, which had been sitting at the repo root the whole time: every line reads desired=12.000 bs=12.000, and bs is io.drive_speed, the value TrackRenderer integrates into position (VehiclePhysicsRun.cpp:595). 12 matches the measured ~11.9. SECOND READING ERROR IN THE SAME PASS: I reported that MASHED_COUPLING_DIAG "emitted nothing", having grepped mashed_re.log — the diag writes to a cwd-relative coupling_diag.log (VehiclePhysicsRun.cpp:606), a different file; then I misread its mtime as stale. Settled properly by backing the file up to verify/realphys_20260821/ and deleting it: it REAPPEARED at 24,734 bytes freshly timestamped, so the diag block is live on the default path. THE PAYOFF, and the reason this correction is worth more than the error cost: the diag localises the defect. Over 220 samples the chain velocity's components change (100 distinct x, 98 distinct z) but its DIRECTION never does — x/z is 0.0203 at the first sample and 0.0210 at the last, so the vector only ever SCALES. velH (velocity heading) is therefore pinned at 1.5498 for the entire run, and yaw follows it because the alignment block at VehiclePhysicsRun.cpp:582-589 steers io.yaw toward the VELOCITY direction rather than from the steer input. The missing term is specifically STEER -> LATERAL VELOCITY. Two consequences: the downstream pieces are not broken (bs tracks desired exactly, 0.012 -> 12.000, and the yaw-align block would follow the velocity if it ever turned), and COLLISION_GATE_BRIEF_D1_2026-07.md:56's "loses forward damping AND turn coupling" is half right on this evidence — emitted speed IS damped correctly to desired; only the steer term is missing, and the unbounded internal integrator is a separate issue that never reaches the car. That is a far narrower search than "the coupling reduction", it is deterministic, and it is instrumented: a fix is directly measurable as velH moving when steer is non-zero. Proof is 0x0049653B lea esi,[ebp+0x7f1038] with ebp = index*0x4C (0x00496536 imul ebp,ebp,0x4c). The first version of the analysis note asserted dword0 and was WRONG for exactly the reason worth recording: the save routine's base address is not the array's base address, and reading one without the other invents a field offset. It also agrees independently with exe_main.cpp already addressing the same two arrays as 0x007f1044 + player*0x4c and 0x007f1504 + player*0x4c, i.e. +0xC of the same bases. OWN-VS-BIND SETTLED BY EVIDENCE: these records are SHARED LIVE STATE, written by 0x00492550 (sets +0x24 = 1 for indices 0..7, bound 0x007F12BC) and by 0x00496568 (38 of 40 captured watchpoint hits, i.e. hot), both independent of the save path. DeserializeFromBuffer was observed firing (ret 0x00409D34) yet the records kept their pre-load pattern while the packed bytes took the file's — the scatter is straight-line code, so the restored values are overwritten by those live writers. A prior survey had classified this row BIND on WEAK role-only evidence; it is now behavioural. ROUND-TRIP ASYMMETRY, new: the gather reads ONE byte (0x00404EF0 mov dl,[eax]) while the restore writes FOUR (0x00404EA0 movzx + 0x00404EA7 mov dword), so a save/load cycle ZEROES the upper 3 bytes of the field. Any port treating both directions as byte-sized is not faithful. RUNTIME CORRELATION IS EXACT: counter 0x00772FFC reads 3, and precisely indices 0,1,2 are both live in C and cleared in A while 3..7 retain the initializer's 1. DELIBERATELY NOT CONCLUDED: a player/controller-slot reading fits every observation (four-slot cap, per-slot liveness handle, the 0x00495xxx/0x00496xxx neighbourhood that also holds the joypad code behind the fix_joypad patch at 0x00495870, a 32-byte buffer at 0x00773058 from 0x004967F2 push 0x20) but it is circumstantial and is recorded as an UNPROVEN HYPOTHESIS, not a finding. HEX CORRECTION: the row recorded the block as "150,076-byte (0x2443C)" — the decimal is right and the hex is arithmetically impossible, 0x2443C being 148,540. The observed size global reads 0x24A3C = 150,076 = exactly 0x928F dwords, so the whole allocation is precisely what the save copies. Layout stays OPEN because nothing populated it in the reachable frontend. ONE CROSS-CHECK WORTH KEEPING: original/gamesave.bin at file offset 0x24F54 holds 00 01 01 01 01 01 01 01 00 00 00 00, byte-for-byte identical to live 0x007F0F54 after the save load, confirming the packed region rides into the file inside the champ span rather than being written separately. PROCESS NOTE: the first watchpoint run died under 0x00496568's write rate, exactly the documented Frida hot-path hazard; a 40-hit cap that disarms the watchpoint once writer identity is captured is what produced the clean data. 2026-08-19  scr1 gate CLOSED by Frida: DAT_007f0f2c is 0 at all 1056 menu reads, and the =1 write is progression-locked behind a counter==12  THE DYNAMIC TRACE MATCHES THE RED EXACTLY, AND THE PORT MODELS THE WRONG KIND OF STATE  Traced the gate on stock original/MASHED.exe (re/frida/trace_savedata_gate.py, hooks off): Interceptor on all three writers of DAT_007f0f2c plus the reader FUN_00432800, logging the flag value at every event, 2211 events over a 16s menu-idle boot. FUN_004927c0 ran 1152 times and set the flag ZERO times, which looked wrong until the decomp showed the write is guarded: DAT_00771980 = DAT_00771980 + 1; if (DAT_00771980 == 0xc) { fill 0x007f0a40..; DAT_007f0f2c = 1; }. The write to 1 only happens when that counter reaches 12, which a fresh menu never does. So DAT_007f0f2c is a PROGRESSION/UNLOCK flag set on a cup or challenge milestone, not a per-boot init. FUN_004924f0 (the zero-fill) ran twice, both before the first read. CONCLUSION, now dynamically proven not just statically argued: the port's model is wrong in KIND. DAT_007f0f2c is runtime progression state whose fresh-menu default is 0 (row greyed) and which flips to 1 only on a milestone; the standalone derives it from the restored save byte at +0x4ec, non-zero in original/gamesave.bin, so it enables a row the original greys. The previous section's open question - which writer runs before the frontend - is answered: NONE do, the flag is just 0. THE FIX is to seed the flag from progression rather than the raw save byte, or minimally default it 0 at the fresh menu and set it only at the same DAT_00771980==0xc milestone. Still a port change against the progression system, not a one-line constant, so it stays out of this pass - but the defect is now fully characterised with dynamic evidence. That is exactly the 3-at-258 / 2-at-282 split, x4 frames = 20 mismatches. So the original disables one row starting at y=258, consistent with avail[3]. THE PORT'S SWITCH ARM IS CORRECT. Reference-manager xrefs on DAT_007f0f2c: exactly ONE reader, 0x004328df in FUN_00432800 (the gate itself), and THREE writers - 0x004305ba in FUN_00430290, 0x00492504 in FUN_004924f0 which assigns 0, and 0x00492991 in FUN_004927c0 which assigns 1. FUN_004924f0 is annotated 'Zero-fills 0xdce9 consecutive DWORDs starting at DAT_007f0f60' and additionally zeroes the flag. So this is RUNTIME state driven by explicit setters. The port derives it ONLY from the restored save span at +0x4ec and models none of the three writers. original/gamesave.bin is a real save (magic 0xDEADBEEF, 0x24FA0 bytes) whose byte at that offset is non-zero, so the standalone enables the row while the original's boot path leaves the flag at 0 and greys it. The divergence is that the original ALSO runs the runtime setters afterwards and the menu reads whatever they last left. Modelling the restore without the setters is the actual bug. [UNCERTAIN] which writer executes before the frontend on a fresh boot. That needs a call-order trace - Frida on the three writers, or a caller walk - and it is the one fact required before changing the port. Deliberately not guessed: forcing avail[3]=0 would turn the gate GREEN while leaving the port wrong for every state where the row SHOULD be enabled, which is a worse outcome than the honest RED. Artifacts verify/orig_screens/s1.bmp and s6.bmp, 640x480 post-Present backbuffer through the d3d9 shim with hooks OFF (stock behaviour), plus the standalone's MASHED_PARITY walk covering all 17 frontend screens in one launch. Pixel comparison: s1 mean 96.47 / 85.40% over threshold, s6 mean 91.08 / 87.70%. THESE ARE NOT A DEFECT MEASURE AND MUST NOT BE QUOTED AS ONE. Two harness asymmetries dominate, both choices rather than rendering failures. (1) MASHED_PARITY clears to a FLAT COLOUR - exe_main.cpp:348-351, g_parity_bg defaults to opaque black and MASHED_PARITY_BG overrides it with a solid ARGB, 'only used in MASHED_PARITY', so dark UI reads against a chosen field - while the original capture has its LIVE VIDEO BACKDROP filling the frame. Comparing a flat clear against a video backdrop accounts for most of the difference by itself. (2) Game state differs: the original was driven from a real boot so its list is populated (Angel Peak, Kharga Temple, Neustein, Timgidski) while the standalone's parity walk pushes the screen without establishing cup state and shows Arctic, Egypt and empty rows. That is the walk not setting up state; whether any data defect also exists CANNOT be decided from this capture. I checked MASHED_PARITY_BG before writing up rather than after, which is the only reason this is recorded as an asymmetry instead of as an 85% rendering failure. Consistent with parity_tooling.md, which makes drawlist_diff.py the primary verifier and pixel diffing the backstop for texture decode and font raster - a pixel percentage across differing backdrops and differing state is not an acceptance signal. WHAT THE IMAGES DO SHOW, by eye on s6: composition matches closely. Challenge Select title and MASHED wordmark in the same places, the selection-highlight bar, the track-preview panel top-right rendering a real track image, four devil icons, and the Select/Back prompt strip. The layout port is in good shape; what differs is backdrop and populated content. Recipe for an acceptance-grade version recorded in the RESULT: capture the standalone in NORMAL mode via MASHED_GOTO=6 so it draws its own backdrop, or equalise the field on both sides; drive both to the SAME game state; and verify with drawlist_diff.py rather than pixels. Not done here - this run's purpose was the focus-stall fix. Evidence verify/parity_20260819/RESULT.md with side-by-side PNGs. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED, no confidence levels moved.
2026-08-19  no_focus_pause PROVEN by measurement, not argument: 15 frames/15s patched vs 1 frame/15s unpatched  THE UNPATCHED GAME IS EFFECTIVELY FROZEN WHEN UNFOCUSED, AND NOW THERE IS A NUMBER FOR IT  Ran a controlled A/B on original/MASHED.exe rather than trusting the patch comment. Metric: the d3d9 shim appends one present_fps line per second to log/fps_limiter.txt under MASHED_FPS_LOG=1 (d3d9_shim.cpp:386-391), so accumulating lines mean Present() is being called and frames are being produced; a main loop parked in WaitMessage() freezes the count. Procedure per arm: spawn, boot 12s, MINIMIZE the game window, sample for 15s. Minimizing was chosen over clicking elsewhere because it is deterministic - the harness asserts GetForegroundWindow() is NOT the game, and in both arms it was not. RESULT: patched (0x996d3 = 0xEB) gained 15 lines in 15s, a full uninterrupted 1/s. Unpatched (0x996d3 = 0x75) gained 1. A 15x difference. Both arms booted normally and were still alive at the end, so this is a rendering-progress difference and not a crash or an early exit. The single line the unpatched arm gained is the partial second before minimize plus whatever stray window message briefly woke WaitMessage. The patch does what its comment claims, and the claim is now MEASURED. This matters because the previous justification for adopting it was a code-reading argument; original-side capture tooling (Frida diffs, MASHED_ORIG_BBDUMP, race_draw_burst.py, re/frida/input_*.py) can now be run unattended with the terminal focused and is known - not assumed - to keep producing frames. STATE RESTORED AND VERIFIED after the control arm: MASHED.exe sha256 b9977dab70607720cf58bc7832937c203b1e0b33ff5896da586baf56aa79fe87, 112 bytes differing from .unpatched, byte at 0x996d3 = 0xeb; .unpatched untouched at BDCAE093. The restore/re-apply cycle also incidentally re-proved the script's --restore path works, which had only been read, never exercised. Harness kept as re/tools/focus_pause_check.py (reusable, tracks and kills only its own PID). Evidence verify/no_focus_pause_20260819/RESULT.md. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED, no confidence levels moved.
2026-08-19  no_focus_pause ADOPTED onto the diffing reference; applied binary patches nine -> ten  ORIGINAL-SIDE CAPTURES NO LONGER STALL WHEN A TERMINAL TAKES FOCUS  Adopted at the user's direction after yesterday's inventory named it the one script of the eleven worth taking. Mechanism: a single signature-guarded byte at 0x004996d3 in the main-loop message pump FUN_00499690, JNZ (0x75) -> JMP (0xEB), so the branch always skips the WaitMessage() call at 0x004996d5. Stock behaviour is that DAT_0077391c (the WM_ACTIVATE focus flag, set by FUN_00499820) reads 0 when unfocused, so every loop iteration blocks until a window message arrives and the game freezes until you click back on it. WHY IT IS SAFE AS A REFERENCE CHANGE, stated explicitly because this binary is the diffing reference: the patch changes WHETHER THE LOOP BLOCKS, not any computed value. DAT_0077391c has exactly one reader, so this is the only pause gate, and no game state is derived from it. Behavioural Frida diffs are unaffected. This is the same class as the already-applied show_windowed and skip_movies, both of which are harness patches on the same binary. VERIFIED, not assumed: the byte diff against .unpatched goes 111/14 clusters -> 112/15, and the ONLY new cluster is a single byte at file 0x996d3 reading 0x75 -> 0xeb; the .unpatched anchor is still BDCAE093A30FBF226BDD852B9C36798A987AEE33B3AE82BF7404B0336EFD3C0E and was not touched; a second run of the script is a clean no-op, so it is idempotent as documented; and MASHED.exe was booted and ran 18 s without crashing, terminated by tracked PID only (no kill-by-name). SCOPE, worth restating so nobody applies this reasoning to the standalone: mashed_re.exe never needed it. exe_main.cpp:1665 already bypasses the focus gate for the demo drivers, which is why the 16-shot capture has been unattended all along. This patch is for ORIGINAL-side runs - Frida diffs, MASHED_ORIG_BBDUMP, race_draw_burst.py, re/frida/input_*.py - which previously stalled whenever the terminal or Frida held foreground focus. Disclosed cost: the main loop spins a core while unfocused. Reversible with --restore. DOC CONSISTENCY: CLAUDE.md moved from Nine to Ten on-disk binary patches in all four places it states the count (the header, the Common-commands comment, the verified-set list, and the not-applied list, which drops from 11 to 10); the byte-diff figures updated to 112/15; the 2026-08-18 audit note annotated to say it describes the pre-adoption state rather than being silently contradicted. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED, no confidence levels moved.

STRINGS EXTRACTOR REWRITTEN, closing the defect that bit my own harvest. re/analysis/strings.txt was produced by a printable-run scanner, which cannot know where a REFERENCED string starts: it records 0x001ce207 '?EgyptPushColumn2' while the code pushes 0x005ce208, one byte later. A lookup keyed on the recorded address fails silently and the name vanishes with no error - that is how the first harvest missed two names. New tool re/tools/dump_strings.py makes the robust primitive explicit: --at <VA> reads forward from an exact address to the first NUL, which is exactly what the code does, so it cannot disagree with the code. Counting mixed-case identifier strings, the PC binary has MORE than either console (PC 906, Xbox 632, PS2 568), so the consoles are a poorer string source, not a richer one. Exactly one plausible function name is Xbox-only, EndVisibilityTest, and it has no PC counterpart string. WHAT THE CONSOLES DID GIVE: content deltas rather than names. PS2-only asset identifiers include BigBoss/BigBoss0-5 (a vehicle name absent from PC, whose roster is Shuriken/Advantage/Stallion/Bullet/Sputter/Cooler/Shorty/Crusader/Creeper/BamBam/Kustom/Formula) and AngelSnowy1/2. Recorded as observations, NOT as a claim that PS2 ships an extra car - the strings could be cut content. AND A GENUINE FIND, THE RWP $Id KEYWORDS: 45 RCS $Id strings naming 40 RenderWare Physics source modules, e.g. //Physics/Rwp37Active/src/core/volume/src/RwpGjk.c. These CONFIRM the hooks.csv label third-party-library[RenderWare-Physics-3.7] exactly (Rwp37Active), and the 40-module set is IDENTICAL across PC, Xbox and PS2, so the middleware is one shared build. One anomaly: RwpBatch.c comes from //Physics/dev/... on PC and Xbox while every other module is Rwp37Active. The strings are UNREFERENCED (0 xrefs, no code unit), so they give a module inventory and a version confirmation but NOT function attribution. Crucially they are present in the PC binary too - this was never console-exclusive and had simply never been looked at. MY OWN DEFECT, FOUND BY THIS: the 70-name harvest resolved names through re/analysis/strings.txt, and that file records some entries with a junk prefix byte absorbed into the string - EgyptPushColumn2 appears as '?EgyptPushColumn2' at 0x001ce207, so a lookup at the true VA failed and the name was invisible. Any name with a prefixed entry was silently skipped. Re-harvested using null-terminated strings read at exact addresses in the binary: 72 pairs across 71 distinct functions, up from 70/69. TWO NAMES RECOVERED: EgyptPushColumn2 -> 0x00449710 (NEW row, C1; body is real code - builds a float vector 200.0/400.0/200.0 and pushes 0x1770) and RoundaboutStopCrane -> 0x0045b350. The second registrar 0x004714f0 therefore has FOUR entries, not two, and they form a symmetric set: EgyptPushColumn1/2, RoundaboutStartCrane/StopCrane. APPARENT TRACKER CONFLICT RESOLVED WITHOUT A RENAME: 0x0045b350 was already tracked as RwInitNullStub at C3, which contradicts the binding table's RoundaboutStopCrane. Read the body: it is a SINGLE ret. So RwInitNullStub is accurate and is KEPT, and the finding is that RoundaboutStopCrane is bound to a shared null stub - accepted by COURSE.LUA and silently ignored, the same pattern as 0x0047ad30. That is the third accepted-and-ignored script command found today. No confidence moved. hooks.csv 5925 -> 5926. Audit re/analysis/binary_claims_audit_20260818.md.

2026-08-18  U-9042 RESOLVED — the vendored Lua is 4.0, not 5.0; 297 hooks.csv rows relabelled and CLAUDE.md corrected  A PROJECT-WIDE LABEL WAS WRONG AND NOBODY HAD CHECKED IT AGAINST THE BINARY  U-9042 asked which Lua type the tag constant 2 denotes, tested at lua_tonumber 0x004b7090. Answering it required the Lua VERSION, which turned out to be mislabelled everywhere. DIRECT EVIDENCE: the image contains "$Lua: Lua 4.0 Copyright (C) 1994-2000 TeCGraf, PUC-Rio $" at 0x005d8790, and it is the ONLY Lua version string present — grep over the full string table for $Lua / Lua N.N / LUA_VERSION / TeCGraf / PUC returns exactly that one line. In Lua 4.0 the type enum is LUA_TUSERDATA 0, LUA_TNIL 1, LUA_TNUMBER 2, so the constant 2 is exactly LUA_TNUMBER. In Lua 5.0 LUA_TNUMBER is 3, which does NOT match the observed test. THREE INDEPENDENT FACTS AGREE: the version string, the tag test at 0x004b7090, and the 16-byte TObject implied by lua_gettop 0x004b6fc0 dividing a pointer difference by 16 (Lua 4.0 lua_TObject = int ttype + 4 pad + 8-byte Number = 16). CONSEQUENCES, both corrections of long-standing claims: (1) 297 hooks.csv rows carried subsystem third-party-library[lua-5.0] and are relabelled third-party-library[lua-4.0], each row annotated with the version-string citation. This is a factual relabel only — NO confidence level moved, up or down. (2) CLAUDE.md:20 stated "Lua 5.x for joypad remap" in the engine-anchor section and is corrected to Lua 4.0 with the string address, so the anchor block no longer misinforms the next session. Nobody had verified either claim against the binary; the enum mismatch only surfaced because U-9042 was filed instead of guessed at when the tag constant looked wrong for the assumed version. That is the whole argument for filing uncertainties rather than smoothing them: the wrong answer here was one plausible sentence away, and it would have propagated into any port of the Lua C API boundary. hooks.csv row count unchanged at 5925. Plate re/analysis/lua_bindings/0x004917a0.md updated in place.
2026-08-18  RainEnable 0x004917a0 C1->C2 + lua_tonumber named + U-9042 filed  RAINENABLE RESETS EVERY RAIN DEFAULT BEFORE RAISING THE FLAG, SO CALL ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING  Second of the two Ghidra-missing functions surfaced by the naming harvest. Read end-to-end, 18 bytes + nop padding, 0x004917a0..0x004917b1: call 0x00490e70; mov dword [0x00771534],1; xor eax,eax; ret. It NEVER reads [esp+4] - it ignores the lua_State entirely, takes no Lua arguments and returns 0 (zero return values), so it is a stateless enable, unlike every sibling RainSet* binding. THE PORT-RELEVANT FINDING IS THE ORDERING: 0x00490e70 already carried [C1 2026-05-14] LineRain parameter init - writes all default values to the global rain parameter cluster (215 bytes, DAT_00616030..37, DAT_006146c0..cc, DAT_00771538, DAT_0077153c). So enabling rain RESETS every rain parameter first, and only then raises the flag. A course script that calls RainEnable AFTER RainSetPosition/RainSetScale/etc would silently discard those settings. Any reimplementation must preserve that order. 0x00771534 identified as the rain-enabled flag from the reference manager: 2 writers (this function at 0x004917a5, FUN_00491530 at 0x00491540) and 7 readers, all inside the 0x00491xxx rain cluster. 0x004b7090 named lua_tonumber: resolves the stack index via FUN_004b6ff0(L,idx), returns *(double*)(p+8), falls back to converter FUN_004b7f30 when the tag at p+0 != 2 and to _DAT_005d1008 when the index does not resolve; returns float10, narrowed by callers through __ftol 0x004a2c48 (already C3). Name only, confidence unchanged. Combined with lua_gettop's >>4 this PINS THE VALUE LAYOUT: TObject is 16 bytes, tag at +0, number at +8 - two independent reads agreeing. U-9042 FILED rather than guessed: the tag constant tested is 2, and which Lua type that denotes is NOT established. hooks.csv labels the island third-party-library[lua-5.0] and CLAUDE.md says Lua 5.x, neither verified against the binary here, so the constant is reported raw instead of mapped to an enum name. Blocks any port of the Lua C API boundary. Also recorded the sibling argument-reading shape for the rest of the family: RainSetHeadColour 0x004917c0 reads args RELATIVE TO STACK TOP (lea esi-3 / esi-2 / esi-1 after lua_gettop), not absolutely, matching the usage string Usage : RainSetTailColour(red,green,blue,alpha) at 0x005cf588. 2026-08-18  U-9041 RESOLVED + 0x0047ad30 C1->C2 + lua_gettop named  THE DOUBLE-REGISTERED HANDLER IS A NO-OP STUB IN THE ORIGINAL  U-9041 asked how one function registered under BOTH Vehicle_Body_Dirt_Texture and Vehicle_Wheel_Dirt_Texture discriminates between them. It does not, because it does nothing. Body read end-to-end, 16 bytes 0x0047ad30..0x0047ad3f: mov eax,[esp+4]; push eax; call 0x004b6fc0; add esp,4; xor eax,eax; ret. The callee return value is DISCARDED (nothing consumes eax between the add and the xor) and the function returns 0, the lua_CFunction convention for zero return values. It never reads a Lua stack value and never writes memory. The two registration sites 0x00440d6d and 0x00440d7c are byte-identical apart from the name pointer, so no index or extra argument is passed: no discriminator exists and none is needed. Both script names are accepted by COURSE.LUA and SILENTLY IGNORED, which means any reimplementation must also do nothing - giving them behaviour would diverge from the original. Where dirt-texture data is actually applied is [UNCERTAIN] and deliberately not speculated. Ghidra had no function at 0x0047ad30 at all, so this was undiscovered code. Promoted C1->C2 on a full end-to-end read; plate re/analysis/lua_bindings/0x0047ad30.md. BONUS from the same read: 0x004b6fc0 named lua_gettop. Body is (L[0]-L[4])>>4 = (L->top - L->base)/sizeof(TObject), and 44 callers - nearly every COURSE.LUA binding handler - call it first to read argc. Confidence left at C2; name only, no ladder move. Also added 0x004714f0 at C1 as the second registrar (2 per-track event registrations: EgyptPushColumn1 0x004496a0, RoundaboutStartCrane 0x0044ca40); its name RegisterTrackEventCallback is DESCRIPTIVE, not recovered from a string, and its body is NOT read. Independent agreement worth recording: 0x0047b980 was ALREADY named RegisterScriptCommand by prior work, which matches this harvest's identification of it as the registrar from call-frequency alone. hooks.csv 5924 -> 5925 rows.

2026-08-16  DrawStreamDump gains a GameFlow-mode tag; D1 result-screen question SETTLED (standalone renderer, no RVA, no ladder move)  THE RESULT SCREEN NEVER RE-RENDERS THE 3D WORLD  Added a mode field to Frame3D, written in DrawStreamDump_Race3DBegin from Race::GameFlow_Mode() and emitted as the first key of each record. Added because the previous capture could not answer the question it was taken for: every counter reports geometry PRESENCE, static once a track loads, so 1000 frames showed one identical state and nothing identified a result-screen frame. Guarded the empty-category case so a frame with no categories cannot emit a trailing comma and invalidate the JSON. MEASUREMENT: window 1:1000 gives {InRace: 1000}; window 1:1100 gives {InRace: 1100}. ZERO Results frames in 1100 consecutive in-race render calls. DrawStreamDump_Race3DBegin is called from TrackRenderer::Render, so if the result screen re-rendered the 3D world it would appear -- it does not appear at all. CONCLUSION: TrackRenderer::Render DOES NOT RUN DURING Results; the 3D world is not re-rendered on the result screen. This DISSOLVES the question rather than answering it. The D3D9-vs-librw difference measured on round2_result/round3_result (~69% of pixels) CANNOT be a difference in how the two renderers draw the world, because on those frames neither draws it. Both earlier hypotheses -- per-channel R/G gain, and D3D9 world-coverage failure -- were asking about a code path that does not execute on the affected frames. WHAT THE RESULT SCREEN THEREFORE IS: whatever the Results state draws (a clear, a fade, an overlay) composited over whatever the backbuffer already held; the two runs differ in what the LAST IN-RACE FRAME left behind and/or in what the results path itself draws. Which of the two is NOT established and is deliberately not guessed at after three wrong diagnoses on this one divergence. COST OF THE MISSING TAG, recorded because the lesson generalises: three failed diagnoses, each internally plausible, each investigating a path that never runs on the affected frames -- (1) per-channel gain from a coincidentally matching blue channel, (2) world-coverage failure from a full-frame sky, (3) the refutation of (2) from world:13 batches on frames that were the wrong frames. One field would have prevented all three. When an instrument answers confidently, check it can see the thing being asked about. Evidence verify/dsproof2/SETTLED.md. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED.
2026-08-16  D1 drawstream refutation WEAKENED (standalone renderer, no RVA, no ladder move)  THE INSTRUMENT CANNOT SEE THE FRAMES IN QUESTION -- "REFUTED" WAS OVERSTATED  Correction to the entry immediately below this one, issued the same day after checking the rest of the capture rather than only the world column. These counters report GEOMETRY PRESENCE, which is static once a track is loaded; they do not vary with what a frame looks like, and they carry NO MARKER identifying which frames are result screens. So the claim "the world is drawn at the result screen" rested on an assumption I did not check: that result-screen frames are among the 1000 captured. Two gaps. (1) The demo is 601-1200 in-race calls and the window captured 1..1000, so frames beyond 1000 -- plausibly including round3_result -- were never observed. (2) Even within the captured range, nothing in the dump lets you point at a frame and say it is round2_result. The librw result frame does render 3D, which is good reason to believe the result screen goes through TrackRenderer::Render and increments this counter, but that is an INFERENCE, not a measurement. REVISED STATUS: the world-coverage hypothesis is NOT refuted, it is UNTESTED -- the instrument as configured cannot observe the specific frames the question is about. The previous entry's "REFUTED" heading claimed more than the data carries and is withdrawn. This does not restore the hypothesis either; it returns it to open. WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT: tag each drawstream record with the GameFlow mode (InRace / Results) -- one field in Frame3D, written in DrawStreamDump_Race3DBegin -- so a result-screen frame is identifiable by name and its world.batches answers the question directly. The line is blocked on instrumentation, not on analysis. STILL SOLID after three retractions on this divergence (per-channel gain, world coverage, and now the refutation of world coverage): the divergence is real, D3D9-side, begins at the round-1/round-2 boundary, and librw is unaffected. 2026-08-16  D1 drawstream at the result frame (standalone renderer, no RVA, no ladder move)  WORLD-COVERAGE HYPOTHESIS REFUTED -- SECOND WRONG DIAGNOSIS IN A ROW, RETRACTED  Ran the decisive test the previous writeup named: MASHED_DBG_DRAWSTREAM3D at the result-screen frame, where world.batches == 0 would have proven the D3D9 world draw was skipping. It does not skip. Window 1:1000 on Arctic under the deterministic race demo gives a world-batch histogram of {13: 1000} with ZERO zero-world frames, and the result screens fall inside that window (the whole demo is 601-1200 in-race calls, bracketed by testing which windows flush). Every in-race render call submits the world, 13 batches, result screens included. THE COVERAGE HYPOTHESIS IS WRONG AND IS RETRACTED -- that is now two failed diagnoses of the same divergence (first a per-channel R/G gain, inferred from a coincidental matching blue channel; then a missing world draw). WHAT THIS LEAVES: the orange fill is drawn OVER a normally-submitted world rather than in place of a missing one. Since only the world-submit path differs between the two runs, whatever draws it must ALSO be present in the librw run, with librw's world submit masking it by draw ORDER -- which would make the orange a defect in BOTH paths that librw merely hides. That is a hypothesis, explicitly untested, and is recorded as such rather than as a finding. WHAT REMAINS SOLID after both retractions: the divergence is real, it is D3D9-side, it begins at the round-1/round-2 boundary, and librw is unaffected. HARNESS NOTES RECORDED for the next attempt, because two runs were wasted on them: MASHED_DBG_DRAWSTREAM3D accepts a:b but the file is ONLY WRITTEN once the counter passes b (DrawStreamDump_Race3DBegin), so a window whose end exceeds the run's total in-race calls produces NO OUTPUT AT ALL, silently -- 1:3000 and 1:1200 both wrote nothing before the range was bracketed. The counter counts IN-RACE RENDER CALLS, not global frames. And the default (=1) is the 60:62 early-race window, which is why every previous look at this data showed a healthy world and said nothing about result screens. Evidence verify/dsproof/RESULT.md. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED.

2026-08-16  D1 R/G hunt (standalone renderer, no RVA, no ladder move)  MY "PER-CHANNEL GAIN" DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG -- IT IS A WORLD-COVERAGE FAILURE  Hunted the supposed R/G gain surviving a round boundary in the D3D9 path. Two hypotheses tested and KILLED: distance fog (MASHED_NO_FOG=1 leaves round2_result identical to 0.1 -- fog is already off on that view because chase_cam is false there, so it was never a candidate) and world relight (MASHED_WORLD_PRELITONLY=1 gives 249.1/112.7/44.7 vs 248.5/111.9/43.9, no effect). Every shading knob did nothing, which is itself the clue. THEN I LOOKED AT THE ACTUAL FRAME AND MY OWN DIAGNOSIS COLLAPSED. I had inferred a per-channel R/G gain from round2_result having B=43.9 on BOTH sides while R and G diverged, and argued a coincidence across two shots was implausible. D3D9_r2.png is a NEAR-UNIFORM SATURATED ORANGE FILL with the standings overlay on top and only faint geometry at the very bottom -- not a washed-out world render at all. The matching blue was a coincidence and I over-read a single number into a mechanism. WHAT THE FRAMES ACTUALLY SHOW: librw renders the world (sunset band, terrain, water, tank prop, car); D3D9 is filled with sky-coloured orange. TrackRenderer draws SKY.DFF FIRST, camera-locked, with ZWRITE OFF, then the world over it -- so a full-frame sky means THE D3D9 WORLD DRAW IS NOT COVERING THE RESULT-SCREEN VIEW from round 2 onward, while librw's world draw does. A coverage/visibility failure, not a shading one, which is exactly why every shading knob was inert. NOT YET ESTABLISHED: why the D3D9 world stops covering that view at the round-2 boundary (culling / a camera the world path does not handle / sectors not re-submitted after a round restart). NEXT STEP IS CONCRETE: MASHED_DBG_DRAWSTREAM3D currently samples only three early frames (f60-f62, where the world draws normally at 13 batches / 49440 verts); getting that dump AT THE RESULT-SCREEN FRAME settles it outright -- batches=0 there proves the skip. STANDING CONCLUSION UNCHANGED: the defect is on the D3D9 side and librw is the stable path on these shots, so inverting MASHED_RENDER_LIBRW would REMOVE a visible defect from the shipping default rather than introduce one -- but it still cannot be justified until faithfulness is adjudicated against the ORIGINAL (U-9039). Evidence verify/d1_measure/BISECT_ROUND_BOUNDARY.md. hooks.csv UNTOUCHED.

The degenerate frame it rested on is confounded by a TRACK MISMATCH -- the pose came from the original driving a QUICK BATTLE (default track) while the standalone rendered ARCTIC. A pose from one track fed to another is meaningless whatever the frame convention, so that evidence cannot support a scale/origin/basis conclusion, and whether any frame mismatch exists is open again. RE-VERIFIED PROPERLY, in-race and deterministic so the backdrop is frozen: 01_inrace_track differs on 96.01% of pixels with the pose set, so the standalone genuinely consumes MASHED_CAM_POSE and repoints its camera. The port works. BUT THE RESULTING VIEW IS DEGENERATE. Pose captured live from the original via race_draw_burst.py: eye=(0.0932,3.2637,4.7482) at=(0.0275,-2.7777,-6.0828). Arctic radius is 80.46 (mashed_re.log R4 track load), so a pose of magnitude ~5 sits at the TRACK CENTRE: the render is the camera buried between two walls, mean RGB (29.5,17.8,11.0) stddev ~13 with 4550 distinct colours, against (145.6,115.3,60.4) stddev ~86 and 42171 colours for the normal chase view. Scale, origin or basis differ between the original camera frame and the standalone world frame; which of the three is not established. FILED AS U-9039 (structural, BLOCKS same-view parity and therefore blocks the D1 faithfulness call). This INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCES ba7c104f (2026-06-19, branch ws-visual-polish, "coord-frame mismatch found", measured there as uniform frames 157.6 on tracks 16/24/26/32 and 8.4 on Arctic) -- a finding that branch carried and that had NEVER been recorded in any tracker on main; the branch was retired 2026-08-15, so filing it now is what keeps it. 2026-08-15  D1 renderer inversion (standalone renderer, no RVA, no ladder move)  MEASURED -> INVERSION BLOCKED ON AN ACCUMULATING DIVERGENCE  With the R10b-fixed gate (16/16 byte-identical across same-build runs, so every delta is signal not harness noise), ran a like-for-like capture differing ONLY in MASHED_RENDER_LIBRW=1. Result: 12 of 16 shots at or near parity (<=0.92%, ten of them <=0.03%), four diverge -- race1/01_inrace_track 71.61%, r6/round3_result 69.15%, r6/round2_result 68.94%, race1/01_action 21.69%. THE KEY PATTERN IS THAT THE DIVERGENCE ACCUMULATES: round1_result 0.01% -> round2_result 68.94% -> round3_result 69.15%, and 01_grid (early race) 0.02% -> 01_action 21.69% -> 01_inrace_track (late race) 71.61%. Parity holds for the FIRST round and the START of a race and degrades after, which is the signature of state that is not reset between rounds or that drifts during a race -- NOT a static difference in how the two renderers shade a frame. Inverting the flag now would make the shipping default a renderer whose output drifts as you play, so D1 inversion is BLOCKED pending that bisect; the round-1/round-2 boundary is a clean bisection point. RECORDED CORRECTION TO MY OWN IN-SESSION READ: on first inspection of 01_inrace_track I read the D3D9 side as blown out with a heavy yellow haze and librw as cleaner, and started to conclude librw was simply better. That is wrong as a general claim -- r5/car_2_drive is VISUALLY IDENTICAL between the two paths (verify/d1_measure/d1_r5_d3d9.png vs d1_r5_librw.png), so the haze is specific to particular viewpoints/late frames rather than a global D3D9 defect. WHAT THE MEASUREMENT DOES NOT ESTABLISH, stated so it is not overread: WHICH RENDERER IS FAITHFUL. Both were compared to EACH OTHER, never to the original; deciding that needs an original-side capture at the same pose, which became possible only today via MASHED_CAM_POSE (standalone consumes a pose) plus the d3d9 shim draw3d.json counters. Evidence verify/d1_measure/ (MEASUREMENT.md + the paired renders).
2026-08-15  R10b residual (standalone renderer code, no RVA, no ladder move)  CLOSED -- CAPTURE GATE NOW ZERO-NOISE 16/16  D1 prerequisite. ROADMAP v3 described R10b as "up to 8 of 13 shots differ between builds of identical source"; that figure was ALREADY STALE when v3 was written -- LIBRW_SIZING_2026-08.md records R10b root-caused 2026-08-01 as ambient DirectInput (device opened DISCL_BACKGROUND|DISCL_NONEXCLUSIVE, so typing in another window flew the camera mid-capture), fixed at 01e87920, reducing 8 unstable shots to 3. Closed the residual today by running the diagnostic that doc had SPECIFIED BUT NEVER EXECUTED: two runs of the SAME build, diff the RELIGHT_CAP headings. Result: headings BIT-IDENTICAL (01_grid 1.54978 / 01_inrace_track 2.03991 / 01_action 1.77192 both runs) while 15 of 16 captures were byte-identical and only 02_back_to_menu differed, on 17.30% of pixels with the right-hand columns at exactly 0.0 -- i.e. the SIMULATION agreed and only a partial-screen backdrop moved, which is the renderer-side branch of that doc's own decision tree. ROOT CAUSE: MpegVideoTexture::Update() reads GetCurrentBuffer() from a live real-time DirectShow graph, and MASHED_DETERMINISTIC pins the frame INDEX not wall-clock, so the backdrop lands on a different video frame depending on how fast the machine reached that point; the TU contained no reference to the deterministic clock at all. FIX: deterministic mode freezes the backdrop after one upload (det_uploaded_ latch in MpegVideoTexture); outside deterministic mode nothing changes and the backdrop animates as before. VERIFIED: two fresh same-build runs are 16/16 BYTE-IDENTICAL (was 15/16), and the frozen menu still shows real video at 93.2% non-black rather than blanking. Evidence verify/r10b/ (A/B pre-fix, C/D post-fix, plus both heading logs). E3' and every pixel-parity claim now have a falsifiable gate on all 16 viewpoints.

2026-08-15  Collision FX (standalone renderer code, no RVA, no ladder move)  SUSPECTED OVER-FIRING INVESTIGATED -> NO DEFECT  The "2164 skids/race vs the 251 the calibration was tuned to" alarm raised earlier today was an INSTRUMENTATION ARTIFACT, not a behaviour change. Root cause: fx_skids_/fx_sparks_ were single counters summed across all four car slots while the MASHED_FX_DEBUG line printed only slot 1 inputs -- so a STATIONARY slot-1 car appeared to emit thousands of skids (log samples 36+ showed spd=0.00 skidI=0.00 with the counter still climbing 20-40 per sample) and no figure was attributable to any car. FIXED: fx_skids_[4]/fx_sparks_[4] per slot, debug line emitted for ALL slots with that slot own inputs and own counters. Re-measured distribution is unremarkable: slot0=772 slot1=358 slot2=241 slot3=378, player highest and genuinely cornering (skidI=2.82 corner=18.72 > kCorner=6.65). The old total was further inflated by MASHED_DRIVE_HOLD extending the race past the natural one the 251 came from -- the two numbers were never like-for-like. LOAD-BEARING CHECK: cumulative emit counts cannot answer "is the rate starving the shared pool", so pool occupancy was instrumented via the existing ParticleSystem::alive() and added to the debug line: 204 samples, min=0 median=130 p90=165 MAX=175 against kPool=1200 (14.6%). No starvation, no recycle pressure. Thresholds STAND at the current scaffold drive model; the D2 re-measure remains for when MASHED_REAL_PHYSICS makes vel[] carry real signal. Evidence verify/fx_verify/fx_debug_perslot_occupancy.txt.

A both-sides-identical failure is a DEAD RUN - neither evidence of a defect nor of correctness - and calling it RED would have been as wrong as calling it GREEN. Re-run on the in-race diff_template.js lane with cache_setter_observe: GREEN 4/4, four distinct fingerprints. TWO DESIGN DECISIONS CARRY THIS RUN. (1) THE OUT POINTER IS AN ABSOLUTE ADDRESS - slot 15's velocity block 0x0088e2b0, which no test below uses as a destination - because cache_setter_observe can only observe absolute addresses, and the payload here is what the function writes THROUGH ITS OUT POINTER. (2) THE MATRIX IS SEEDED TO IDENTITY, along with the source vector per test. Without seeding, determinism would rest on the two back-to-back calls observing the same live vehicle state, and a frame boundary landing between them would produce a spurious RED - a flaky test being its own kind of false evidence. Identity was chosen because it is LAYOUT-AGNOSTIC: the same bit pattern whether the matrix is row-major or column-major, so the run makes no assumption about a storage order that has not been established. It also makes the output equal the seeded input, so every fingerprint is directly readable: idx0 -> 1.0/2.0/3.0, idx1 -> 4.0/5.0/6.0, idx2 -> -1.5/0.25/8.0, and idx16 (out of range) -> the out block's 0x5EED0000/1/2 sentinel SURVIVED INTACT, proving the bounds check bites and nothing was written. A port with a wrong stride, a wrong source offset or a wrong copy offset shows up immediately against those. BOTH SIDES CALL THE SAME ORIGINAL FUN_004c3df0, so the transform itself is not under test - what is under test is the bounds check, the three address computations and the copy, i.e. exactly what out3_idx could not see. Contrived state, C3-grade: every seeded and observed address including the world matrix is snapshotted and restored by the handler, so the live race is unperturbed after the run. C3 not C4 - a synthetic A/B on seeded state is not a canonical scenario. And ADD ESP,0x24 at 0x005515f6 is a SINGLE COMBINED CLEANUP FOR TWO __cdecl CALLS - call 2 pushes 5 dwords and call 3 pushes 4, and 9 dwords is 0x24 - while only call 1 gets its own ADD ESP,0x10 at 0x005515b6; a port cleaning up after each call separately would unbalance the stack. (3) THREE CONSTANTS WERE CITED TO THE FUNCTION START rather than to the instruction referencing them, which is not a citation - the same defect the iter20 __ftol audit found across 24 plates. All constants now carry their own instruction address. The two rows the NEEDS_NEW_HANDLER re-screen freed, both authored from the raw listing and both verified WITHOUT WRITING A HANDLER - which was the point of the re-screen. 0x004b6b00 is three bytes, `MOV dword ptr [ECX],EAX ; RET`, and eax_ecx_insert drives exactly that; 0x00407550 is an ESI-keyed linear scan and esi_global_search needed one additive defaulted field (key_off) because it seeded the key at entry+0 while this function compares at +0x44. BOTH FIRST RUNS WERE FALSE GREENS AND BOTH ARE RECORDED, because they are the same defect class in two different disguises and it is the class this session keeps hitting. (1) 0x004b6b00: eax_ecx_insert ignores its test list and puts bufA's ADDRESS in EAX, so every vector produced an identical observation. orch_preflight CAUGHT THIS BEFORE THE RUN - 'path1_tests has 1 vector(s); cannot be non-degenerate' - and rather than paper over the gate the handler gained cfg.eax_from_test (default false, edx_val being the existing precedent for a raw-immediate register value), so each vector now stores a distinct value and *ECX reads back exactly it. (2) 0x00407550: key_off was in the registry entry and in the handler, and STILL did nothing, because early_window_leaf_diff builds cfg from an EXPLICIT ALLOWLIST and the key was not on it. A dropped key does not error - the handler sees undefined, which reads as 'feature off'. So the seed went to entry+0, every vector missed, and BOTH SIDES RETURNED 0: GREEN 4/4 that a port whose entire body was `return 0` would have passed just as well. THIS IS THE EARLY-WINDOW TWIN OF THE RULE orch_preflight ALREADY ENFORCES FOR run_diff.py - present in the entry is NOT enough, the driver must FORWARD it - and preflight does not check this lane, which is now a known gap. Fixed by forwarding key_off, eax_from_test and reseed_per_side into cfg. This row had been spec'd SPEC_COMPLETE and authorable by a handler brief; it is `sprintf` from the MSVC CRT and must not be ported. The identification is unambiguous from the disassembly — it builds a fake `FILE` on the stack (`[EBP-0x20]` `_ptr` = dst, `[EBP-0x18]` `_base` = dst, `[EBP-0x1c]` `_cnt` = 0x7fffffff, `[EBP-0x14]` `_flag` = 0x42), takes the varargs pointer with `LEA EAX,[EBP+0x10]`, and hands all three to `_output` (FUN_004a504f, already recorded as the printf format core). On return it NUL-terminates via `AND byte ptr [EAX],0` at 0x004a2ba0, or calls FUN_004a4ea8 on overflow, and returns the character count in EDI. That is the textbook MSVC sprintf shape. Decisive corroboration: 0x004a2b60 falls inside the FidDB-attested library band `msvc-crt-main span=0x004a0000..0x004b4000`, which the hooks.csv row for 0x004a4ea8 explicitly marks "do NOT promote". THE GENERAL DEFECT, which is the reason this is worth a CHANGELOG entry: **a screening brief asks whether a function can be TESTED; it never asks whether the function should be PORTED.** Nothing upstream applied a library-band filter, so six gate-pool rows sat inside the CRT band and only the two already carrying a LIBRARY_SKIP tag had been caught — 0x004a2b60, 0x004a2c2f, 0x004af32d and 0x004a77eb were all being processed as ordinary port candidates. 0x004a77eb is the sharpest case: re/orchestrator/gate_verdicts_manual.tsv had called it "the one row of the twelve genuinely still unscreened", when in fact it is library residue and never needed screening at all. Pool tally moves accordingly: DEFER 2 -> 6, NEEDS_NEW_HANDLER 22 -> 20, NEEDS_GHIDRA 31 -> 30, MUTATOR_LANE 44 -> 43. 2026-07-31  0x00482900  FUN_00482900  C2 (plate + hooks.csv note CORRECTED, no C-level change)  Orchestrator iter17. Not a promotion — a factual correction to an existing C2 whose mechanical description was materially wrong. The old plate and hooks.csv note both described `int FUN_00482900(void)` — zero arguments — with `FUN_004a2c48` as a "checkpoint/frame count getter". Both claims are false. `FUN_004a2c48` is **`__ftol`**, the MSVC CRT x87 round-to-int64 helper, already C3 and byte-identical at Math/FPURound.cpp (hooks.csv:254). It takes its input in **ST0**, so Ghidra — which cannot see x87 register arguments — rendered the call as `FUN_004a2c48()` and, because this function's own two arguments are consumed only by x87 instructions, reported the whole function as void-parameter. The real signature is `int f(float param_1, int param_2)`: `FLD [ESP+4]` / `FMUL [0x005cc728] (=60.0f)` / `FIDIV [ESP+0xc]` / `CALL __ftol` / `LEA ESI,[EAX+EAX*8]` / `LEA ESI,[ESI*4+0x19c]`, i.e. `frames = (int)(param_1 * 60.0f / param_2)` then `size = frames*36 + 412`. Note the `*36` is a strength-reduced LEA pair, not an `imul`, and the divisor is read with FIDIV so param_2 is an INTEGER. The old plate's arithmetic conclusion (`count*36+412`) was right; its derivation was not — the count is COMPUTED from two arguments, not fetched from anywhere. A port written from the old plate would have been `int f(void)` calling a getter that does not exist. Found by the iter16 clause re-screen, which flagged two plates contradicting each other about FUN_004a2c48 ("checkpoint/frame count getter" vs a bare [STUB]); hooks.csv turned out to agree with neither. Both plates corrected; the secondary one (breadth_unmapped_0048x) additionally cited the call at 0x00482904, which is the `PUSH ESI` — the call is at 0x0048290f. SYSTEMIC NOTE, not fixed here: 246 plates under re/analysis/ contain the zero-argument form `FUN_004a2c48()`. That rendering is expected and not itself an error — it is the decompiler hiding an ST0 argument. The defect is only in plates that go on to INVENT a semantic for it; 25 plates correctly identify it as ftol. Detection recipe for a future sweep: `grep -rl "FUN_004a2c48()" re/analysis/` intersected with plates that do NOT mention ftol, then check each for an assigned meaning. The meaning of param_1/param_2 is left [UNCERTAIN] — the 60.0f is consistent with a seconds-to-frames conversion, but nothing in this body names either argument, so it must be resolved from the three call sites.

2026-07-31  0x005b10a0 + 0x005b10e0  AudioTableDispatchAt4 / AudioTableDispatchAt8  C2->C3  Orchestrator iter15. A dispatch pair that is BYTE-IDENTICAL apart from one displacement byte — `mov ecx,[eax+4]` at 0x005b10a8 vs `mov ecx,[eax+8]` at 0x005b10e8 — so one handler with a CONFIG value covered both: `entry = *(holder + K) + idx*8; entry[0](a1, *(u16*)(entry+4), a4)`. Each 8-byte table entry carries a function pointer at +0 and a 16-bit datum at +4 passed as the dispatched call's MIDDLE argument; nothing names that datum, so it is reported as a raw u16 with no meaning assigned. ARGUMENT ORDER was read off the stack rather than from the decompiler: the two PUSHes before `mov eax,[esp+0xc]` (0x005b10bb) shift ESP, so that read reaches the ORIGINAL [esp+4] — a1 is pushed LAST and is therefore the dispatched call's arg1, a4 pushed FIRST and is arg3. The `xor eax,eax` / `mov ax` pair zero-extends the u16; it is never sign-extended. NEW ARG_TYPE `vtable_table_dispatch`: nothing real is dispatched — the handler writes its own NativeCallback into entry[0], so the call lands on a recorder, and the recorded triple proves the *8 stride, the +4 aux read, the indirection offset and the argument order in a single observation. 2026-07-26  0x00418a00 (+ 0x00421060)  StructInit418a00 / StructZero421060  BUGFIX (stays C2)  SIXTH confirmed boot-AV crasher — register-arg ABI mismatch, third instance of the class. FUN_00418a00 reads its struct pointer ONLY from ESI (0x00418a02 `PUSH ESI` feeding the memset, then 0x00418a0d/0x00418a10 `MOV [ESI+8|0xc],EAX` and 0x00418a18..0x00418a21 `MOV [ESI+0x10..0x13],AL`); the game's callers push NOTHING. The reimpl was `void __cdecl StructInit418a00(unsigned char* p)`, so the installed inline-JMP took a caller stack-garbage word as `p` and wrote 0x6c bytes through it. The source comment even documented the register ABI and then dismissed it ("the diff drives the original via an ESI trampoline and the reimpl via a stack-arg pointer") — that reasoning is about the HARNESS's call, never about the installed hook's real callers. FIXED by a naked marshalling shim StructInit418a00_RegAbi (`push esi; call StructInit418a00; add esp,4; ret`) which is what RH_ScopedInstall now registers; the C body keeps its stack ABI so re/frida/hooks_registry.py is unaffected. EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=0x00418A00 BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 @ 6.88/7.23/7.23 s; AFTER = 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. ALSO FIXED on disassembly evidence alone: 0x00421060 StructZero421060 is the identical pattern (0x00421062 `PUSH ESI`, 0x0042106d..0x00421076 `MOV [ESI+n],EAX`, nothing on the stack) but does NOT AV in isolation (3/3 BOOTS 16 s both before and after) — a wrong ABI that happens to land on writable garbage is still a wrong ABI. Found by bisect_hook_index.ps1 index 1023.
2026-07-26  0x0042ad90  Find42ad90  BUGFIX (stays C2)  FIFTH confirmed boot-AV crasher — register-arg ABI mismatch. FUN_0042ad90 takes ALL THREE args in registers and nothing on the stack: 0x0042ad90 `TEST EDX,EDX` (arr), 0x0042ada6 `CMP EAX,EBX` (key), 0x0042adaa `CMP EDI,ESI` (n). The reimpl was `std::uint32_t __cdecl Find42ad90(arr,key,n)`, so the installed hook read three words of caller stack garbage and walked a wild pointer hunting the 0xff070000 sentinel (0x0042ad9f / 0x0042adb4). FIXED by a naked marshalling shim Find42ad90_RegAbi (`push edi; push ebx; push edx; call Find42ad90; add esp,0Ch; ret`) registered in place of the C body; EBX/ESI/EDI are callee-saved under __cdecl so the shim preserves exactly what the original preserves (it only pushes/pops ESI at 0x0042ad9a/0x0042adbe), and the return stays in EAX. The C body keeps its stack ABI because hooks_registry.py drives the export `Find42ad90` with stack args (arg_type edx_ebx_edi_find sets registers on the ORIGINAL side only). EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=0x0042AD90 BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 @ 8.15/8.40/8.42 s; AFTER = 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. Found by bisect_hook_index.ps1 index 951. 2026-07-26  0x004b40c0 (+ 0x004b4140)  RenderElemArrayCopy / RenderElemArrayCopyAll  BUGFIX (stays C2)  FOURTH confirmed boot-AV crasher, RESOLVED — a LOST RETURN VALUE, a new sub-class of the ABI-mismatch family. The prior session correctly refused to guess: the body was faithful to the cited ASM and the dump signature differed (eip=0x004b52ac, WRITE violation on 0x004a467c inside MASHED's own code/.rdata, not the near-null +0x44 dispatch). Ghidra resolves it exactly. FUN_004b40c0 loads `MOV EAX,dword ptr [EDX+0x24]` at 0x004b40c4 — the element COUNT — before any branch, and NO path afterwards clobbers EAX (the loop counter is ECX at 0x004b40e0, the scratch is EDI), so EAX still holds the count at the RET at 0x004b40e7: the function implicitly RETURNS the count. That value propagates through the tail-JMP adjustor FUN_004b4130 (0x004b413b `JMP 0x004b40c0`) and through the wrapper FUN_004b4140 (whose only post-CALL instruction is `ADD ESP,0x8` at 0x004b414c). The SOLE caller of 0x004b4140 is FUN_004b5260, which consumes it at 0x004b5281 `MOV ESI,EAX` and uses it as the trip count of the write loop at 0x004b52a3..0x004b52af — where 0x004b52ac is `MOV dword ptr [EDX+0x4],EDI`, the exact faulting instruction from the dump. Both reimpls were declared `void`, leaving EAX undefined, so FUN_004b5260 iterated a garbage count, read pointers off the end of its 0x44-byte frame and wrote through them. FIXED: RenderElemArrayCopy is now `int __cdecl` returning `count`, RenderElemArrayCopyAll is now `int __cdecl` forwarding it. EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 (prior session, ~6.6 s); AFTER = 0x004b40c0 3/3 BOOTS 16 s and 0x004b4140 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. LESSON: a `void` reimpl is only safe when the original's EAX is provably dead at every RET; an original that merely leaves a useful value in EAX has an implicit return that the callers may depend on.

2026-07-26  0x005ab380  AudioRwsChunkHeaderRead  BUGFIX (stays C2)  **ROOT CAUSE of the pc=0x44 boot AV, found and FIXED.** The port was an 8-byte STACK BUFFER OVERFLOW: the original reads 0xc bytes into the lowest of three CONTIGUOUS stack slots (-0xc/-0x8/-0x4), so `read(&local_c, 0xc)` legitimately fills all three; the transcription copied that idiom literally into three SEPARATE C++ locals and passed `&local_c`. C++ guarantees nothing about the placement/order/padding of distinct locals, so the callee wrote 12 bytes into a 4-byte object, smashing 8 bytes of the caller's frame, while local_8/local_4 were read effectively uninitialised. Fixed by reading into an explicit `std::uint32_t hdr[3]` (contiguous by construction) and copying out. LESSON: a stack layout the original relies on must be modelled EXPLICITLY, never inferred from adjacent local declarations. Repo-wide sweep found this exact pattern in only ONE place. EVIDENCE (single-hook before/after, MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=AudioRwsChunkHeaderRead, WaitForExit-measured): BEFORE = AV 0xC0000005 @ 4.71 s / 4.61 s; AFTER = BOOTS 18.02 s / 18.00 s. HOW IT WAS FOUND: static analysis (Ghidra) pinned the faulting instruction to 0x005ab237 `CALL dword ptr [EAX+0x44]` in the RW-audio stream-handler command dispatcher, validated by a dual-register match against the dump (eax=0x02e0c884=obj, ecx=0x0000080a=[[obj]+0xc]); the object was a VALID heap pointer with only its +0x44 handler slot garbage — consistent with a neighbouring frame being smashed. Independently, a hook-INDEX binary search using the built-in MASHED_HOOK_LO/HI knobs converged on index 86 = 0x005AB380 = this function. Static and dynamic evidence agreed. ALSO CORRECTED: the 2026-07-25 conclusion that subset installs 'do not dodge it' is FALSE — full .asi with MASHED_HOOK_HI=0 (zero hooks installed) BOOTS 18 s, so the trigger is hook INSTALLATION, not .asi static-init.
